# Project Plan

Coordination blackboard for ORCH (Claude Code), GB (Grok Build), CX (Codex AI), S5 (Sonnet 5 headless).
Rules: `AGENTS.md` (summary) and `docs/COORDINATION_PROTOCOL.md` (authoritative).
Status lifecycle: `pending → claimed → in_progress → needs_review → done`, `blocked` from claimed/in_progress. Builders never set `done`.
control.mode is **strict**: builders never edit PLAN.md — the dispatcher claims, the supervisor applies state from your fenced `devteam-control` block (docs/CONTROL.md).

## Work Items

### TASK-002
**Title:** OIK-011/012/013 (local scope) — Postgres 16 + pgvector compose, schema v1 migrations, append-only audit
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §1; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E2 OIK-011, OIK-012, OIK-013; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Platform_Synthesis_Spec_v0.1.md §5.1; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §8
**Owned_Paths:** infra/compose/**, infra/postgres/**
**Depends_On:** —
**Description:** Data-layer bootstrap, deliberately Node-free so it runs concurrently with TASK-001 (no package.json, no npm deps — plain SQL + shell/psql + docker compose only; the typed query layer is TASK-006). Deliver: (1) `infra/compose/docker-compose.local.yml` — Postgres 16 with pgvector, localhost-bound, named volume; plus a `docker-compose.prod.yml` variant whose port binding is written for Tailscale-interface-only exposure with a comment noting live verification happens on clawsrv (ops step, out of this task's scope — Handover §8). (2) Numbered SQL migrations in `infra/postgres/migrations/` (NOT under packages/ — territory isolation from TASK-001's workspace scaffold; TASK-006's typed layer will consume them from there) implementing Synthesis Spec §5.1 exactly: enums task_status, run_status, risk_tier, approval_status; tables tasks, runs, capabilities, role_grants, approvals, audit_events, profile_facts, knowledge_chunks; all §5.1 indexes. (3) Append-only enforcement on audit_events (OIK-013): rules/triggers making UPDATE and DELETE provable no-ops. (4) `infra/postgres/scripts/` — apply/verify shell scripts (psql-based, idempotent re-run) and a SQL test script proving the append-only property and pgvector availability. Schema questions: Synthesis §5.1 is authoritative for v1 — do not improvise columns; a §5.1 ambiguity is a SPEC_AMBIGUITY block, not a judgment call.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] `docker compose -f infra/compose/docker-compose.local.yml up -d` yields a healthy Postgres 16 with `CREATE EXTENSION vector` live (WBS OIK-011 "pgvector extension live")
- [x] Apply script creates every §5.1 enum, table, and index; re-running it is a no-op (WBS OIK-012 "idempotent"); down/reversal scripts exist per migration (WBS OIK-012 "reversible")
- [x] UPDATE and DELETE on audit_events are provable no-ops — test script demonstrates row survives both, and the attempt is not an error that breaks callers (WBS OIK-013)
- [x] Prod compose variant binds Postgres to a Tailscale-only interface by config, with the clawsrv live-verification step documented as deferred ops (WBS OIK-011 acceptance noted as environment-bound; Handover §8)
- [x] Zero Node/npm dependencies introduced anywhere in Owned_Paths (territory-isolation constraint, this plan)
- [x] No credentials anywhere — compose uses env-var references with a `.env.example` carrying obvious placeholders only (N4)

### TASK-003
**Title:** OIK-017/018 — packages/shared: canonical JSON + sha256 action digest
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §1, §4; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-017, OIK-018; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §4.3
**Owned_Paths:** packages/shared/src/**, packages/shared/test/**, packages/shared/README.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-001
**Description:** The single canonical-JSON + digest implementation for the whole platform (N10 — no reimplementation will ever be permitted elsewhere, so precision here is everything; this is why the task goes to the careful-reading builder). Implement per Handover §4.3: canonical form = sorted keys, no whitespace, UTF-8, numbers in shortest round-trip form; `action_digest = sha256(canonicalJson({toolName, input, destination}))`. Document the byte-level spec in packages/shared/README.md (WBS OIK-018 "documented byte-level spec") — edge cases explicitly: unicode normalization stance, -0 vs 0, exponent form, null vs absent keys, nested arrays/objects, non-finite numbers (reject). Node crypto only — zero runtime dependencies. packages/shared's package.json/tsconfig belong to TASK-001; if a manifest change is essential, block with OWNERSHIP_CONFLICT rather than editing it.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Property tests prove stability under key order, unicode content, number forms, and nesting — same value object always yields byte-identical canonical JSON and digest (WBS OIK-017 acceptance)
- [ ] Digest reproducible across processes: test spawns a child process and compares digests (WBS OIK-018 "reproducible across processes")
- [ ] No collisions across the fixture set; fixtures contain no credential-like values (WBS OIK-017; N4)
- [ ] Non-finite numbers and undefined reject with a typed error, not silent coercion (fail-closed posture, directive §4)
- [ ] README byte-level spec covers every edge case above (WBS OIK-018)
- [ ] Zero runtime dependencies; strict types, no `any` (CLAUDE.md)
**Branch:** task/TASK-003-s5
**Started_At:** 2026-08-14T15:08:45Z

- --- prior REWORK findings (2026-08-15T06:24Z), retained for the record ---
- [BLOCKING — AC3 not enforced] The `branches: 100` coverage threshold is never evaluated by any automated path, so the gate is a no-op in practice. `packages/policy/package.json` sets `"test": "vitest run"` (no `--coverage`); root `pnpm test` = `pnpm -r test` runs exactly that; the CI `test` job runs `pnpm test`; and `vitest.config.ts` `coverage` block omits `enabled: true`. Vitest only collects coverage and enforces thresholds when coverage is explicitly enabled, so the 100%-branch bar fires ONLY under a manual `vitest run --coverage` that nothing invokes. Net effect: a future edit to packages/policy that introduces an uncovered branch passes CI green — the exact regression this WBS acceptance criterion exists to prevent, on a governance-core protected module. FIX (in-territory, both paths are in your Owned_Paths): either set `packages/policy` `"test": "vitest run --coverage"`, OR add `enabled: true` to the `coverage` block in `vitest.config.ts`, so `pnpm test`/CI enforces the 100%-branch threshold on every run. (@vitest/coverage-v8 is already installed, so CI has the provider.) Independently confirmed the threshold DOES fail closed once coverage is actually collected — introducing an uncovered branch drops branches to 90% and exits 1 — so ONLY the wiring is missing, not the gate logic.
- [BLOCKING — evidence not reproducible] The pasted Test_Evidence command `pnpm --filter @oikonomos/policy test -- --coverage` does not measure coverage: everything after `--` is forwarded to the `test` script, where vitest treats `--coverage` as a positional test-name filter, not the coverage flag. Run literally it exits 0 with 9 passed and NO coverage table — yet the evidence claims "100% statements/branches/functions/lines". The claimed numbers did not come from the command as recorded. Re-run with coverage actually enabled (e.g. `pnpm --filter @oikonomos/policy exec vitest run --coverage`, or the corrected `test` script) and paste the real coverage table. Same root cause as the finding above — coverage was never being collected by the recorded command.
- [NON-BLOCKING — no rework, ORCH-owned] Cross-document contradiction discovered, recorded here so it is not lost: Handover §4.2 (which you correctly implemented) says `effectiveTier = max(default_tier, roleGrantOverride)`, more-restrictive-wins, "No path grants a role a lower tier than a capability's default" — but Synthesis Spec §5.1 comments `role_grants.max_tier` as "role ceiling; broker takes min(default, ceiling)", the OPPOSITE direction (a ceiling that LOWERS the tier). Per CLAUDE.md document precedence (Handover v1.0 > Synthesis v0.1) Handover governs and your `max()` is correct — no change to TASK-007. ORCH action item (not builder): author an ADR recording that Handover §4.2 supersedes the Synthesis §5.1 min/ceiling comment, and flag that feeding the DB `role_grants.max_tier` column (named/commented as a ceiling) into this `roleGrantOverride` parameter of a `max()` function would fail OPEN — the future broker/role-grant wiring task must not do so blindly.
- [2026-08-15T08:55:00Z] [ORCH] ADR-003 authored (docs/decisions/ADR-003-tier-resolution-direction.md) closing the non-blocking cross-document contradiction the reviewer found: Handover section 4.2 max() governs, Synthesis section 5.1 min/ceiling comment superseded, so YOUR implementation is correct and needs no change. The ADR also records the fail-open trap for the future broker wiring ticket: role_grants.max_tier is a CEILING and must never be passed raw as the roleGrantOverride floor. Your two BLOCKING findings stand and are both in-territory; resume on task/TASK-007-cx and fix them. ### TASK-008
**Title:** OIK-004/007 fast-follow — CI scanners must honor .gitignore (walk.mjs) ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4, §6; docs/decisions/ADR-002-permission-bypass-ban-scope.md Amendment A; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E1 OIK-004, OIK-007
**Owned_Paths:** infra/ci/lib/**, infra/ci/test-banned-modes.mjs, infra/ci/test-secret-scan.mjs
**Depends_On:** TASK-004
**Description:** Backlog (TBD — **GB or CX only, never S5**: protected path infra/ci, different-model review rule per directive §3). Raised by the opus-4-8 reviewer as a non-blocking fast-follow on TASK-004 and confirmed live by ORCH at 21:10Z: `infra/ci/lib/walk.mjs` walks the whole tree without honoring .gitignore, so a local full-tree run of `banned-modes.mjs` and `secret-scan.mjs` both exit 1 on `.devteam/runs/*.log` — machine-local builder transcripts that legitimately contain bypass tokens and API-key-shaped fixtures. CI on a clean checkout and the pre-commit staged path are both unaffected, which is why this is not a TASK-004 rework, but a scanner that cries wolf locally is a scanner developers learn to ignore — and that is how a real finding gets missed. ORCH has separately added `.devteam/` to .gitignore (the untracked-but-not-ignored state was a genuine N4 exposure: one `git add .` would have committed credential-shaped content); this task makes the scanners agree with git about what is part of the repo. Implement gitignore-awareness in walk.mjs (`git check-ignore --stdin` when inside a work tree, with a documented fallback skip-list when git is absent so CI containers without git still behave). Do NOT weaken Amendment A's enforcement surfaces: an ignored path is skipped because it is not part of the repo, never because it is carved out.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `node infra/ci/banned-modes.mjs` and `node infra/ci/secret-scan.mjs` both exit 0 on the real working tree with .devteam/ present (the live failure this task exists to fix)
- [ ] A planted violation in a TRACKED enforcement path is still caught — proven by test, so the fix cannot have been achieved by over-skipping (WBS OIK-004/007)
- [ ] A planted violation in an IGNORED path is skipped, and the test asserts the reason is gitignore-membership, not an allowlist carve-out (ADR-002 Amendment A)
- [ ] Documented fallback when git is unavailable; behavior identical on a clean checkout with no .devteam/
- [ ] Existing self-tests (6/6 banned-modes, 9/9 secret-scan) stay green
**Branch:** task/TASK-008-gb
**Started_At:** 2026-08-14T21:17:41Z
**Progress_Notes:**

### TASK-009
**Title:** Workspace dependency integration — E3 manifests + lockfile (single-owner, cross-cutting)
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** critical

**Depends_On:** —
**Description:** Protocol §4 single-owner integration task for the one file every other E3 task would otherwise contend for. `pnpm-lock.yaml` is a single shared file: three concurrent packages each adding a dependency would all need it, and intersecting Owned_Paths is illegal. Rather than serialising the whole epic behind that, this task adds every workspace dependency the E3 wave needs in ONE pass, and the implementation tasks then own only `src/**` and `test/**`. Declare with the `workspace:*` protocol: approvals → @oikonomos/shared + @oikonomos/db; audit → @oikonomos/shared + @oikonomos/db; broker → @oikonomos/shared + @oikonomos/policy + @oikonomos/approvals + @oikonomos/audit. Add NO external runtime dependency — nothing in this wave needs one (pg already lives in packages/db, and the broker ships as a library handler here, not an HTTP server; the Fastify surface is OIK-084/E9). **Write zero implementation code.** This task exists because TASK-005, TASK-006 and TASK-007 each lost time to a missing manifest grant; it converts that recurring block into one deliberate task.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] Each of the three manifests declares its workspace dependencies using the `workspace:*` protocol, matching the package boundaries in Build Handover §3
- [x] `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` passes on the committed lockfile
- [x] `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm -r build`, `pnpm -r test` and `pnpm lint` all still exit 0 — this task changes wiring, not behaviour
- [x] `git diff --stat master...HEAD` shows ONLY the three manifests, the lockfile, and this task's dossier — zero files under any `src/` or `test/`

### TASK-011
**Title:** OIK-025 — packages/audit: append-only writer
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4, §5; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-025; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Platform_Synthesis_Spec_v0.1.md §5.1 (audit_events); docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md R3
**Owned_Paths:** packages/audit/src/**, packages/audit/test/**, packages/audit/vitest.config.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-009, TASK-020
**Description:** The append-only audit writer over the `audit_events` table TASK-002 created. Not a protected path, which is why it is assignable to S5. Two properties carry the weight: **every decision is written, including denials** — a denial that is not audited is indistinguishable from an action that never happened — and **a write failure fails the action closed** (ADR-001 R3), so the caller must be able to tell "audited" from "not audited" and refuse to proceed on the latter. Never swallow a write error. TASK-002 already enforces append-only in the database via `DO INSTEAD NOTHING` rules; your job is the writer side, and your tests should prove the property holds end-to-end from this package rather than re-testing the SQL in isolation. Integration tests run against the local compose Postgres (`infra/compose/docker-compose.local.yml`, apply `infra/postgres/migrations/001_schema_v1.up.sql`); follow TASK-006's pattern of skipping cleanly when `DATABASE_URL` is unset so the suite stays green on a machine with no database. **N4 is absolute here:** no credentials in fixtures, and audit payloads are exactly the place a careless test fixture leaks one.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Every decision is written including denials — test asserts a denial produces a row, not just an allow (WBS OIK-025 "Every decision written incl. denials")
- [ ] A write failure fails the action closed: the writer surfaces the failure to its caller rather than returning success or swallowing it (WBS OIK-025 "write failure fails the action closed"; ADR-001 R3)
- [ ] UPDATE and DELETE against a written event are provable no-ops when exercised through this package (WBS OIK-013 property, verified from the writer side)
- [ ] Integration tests pass against the compose Postgres and skip cleanly with no `DATABASE_URL` (TASK-006 precedent)
- [ ] No credential-like values in any fixture; `node infra/ci/secret-scan.mjs` exits 0 (N4)
**Branch:** task/TASK-011-s5

Did not guess an API. Branch retained at d86c4ba (dossier only).

WHERE event_id = $1 asserts rowCount 0, a re-read asserts the actor and payload are unchanged, and DELETE is proven symmetrically. N4 is exemplary - every fixture is FRAGMENT-ASSEMBLED at runtime (["AKIA","EXAMPLEFAKEKEY01"].join("")) so no contiguous credential-shaped literal exists in source, and every value carries PLACEHOLDER/FAKE/EXAMPLE text; the same discipline is applied to the detector's own regex patterns. TASK-011's tests are byte-unchanged and still pass. NARROW GRANT HELD EXACTLY: only pg ^8.23.0 as a devDependency, the corresponding lockfile lines, nothing else - and packages/audit/src/** still contains zero raw SQL and no pg import, so the runtime path stays through @oikonomos/db precisely as scoped. insertAuditEvent is still not re-exported, so redaction cannot be bypassed. MINOR CONSISTENCY NOTE, not a finding against the work: the redaction integration test asserts on the writer's return value rather than issuing its own SELECT. I judged this MEETS the criterion - it says "asserting on the row actually persisted, not on the middleware's return value", and the returned object is the row Postgres sent back through RETURNING after a jsonb round-trip, so it is server data and not the middleware's in-memory copy. Worth noting only because the append-only test next door demonstrates the stronger re-read pattern. **PROTOCOL FINDING - DOSSIER WRITTEN BUT NOT COMMITTED:** dossiers/TASK-012.md exists in the worktree with 34 lines and three preflight references, and was never committed, so the branch does not carry it and the merge did not bring it across. Handover §4.3, the GOVERNING document under CLAUDE.md precedence, says nothing about the render at all, and this task's AC1 requires only that the row "carries" it. THE EXPOSURE: `action_digest` binds the PAYLOAD (toolName, input, destination); it does NOT bind the RENDER. A payload mutated after grant is caught by digest mismatch (OIK-023/TASK-015). But a render that is MISLEADING AT ISSUANCE produces a perfectly valid digest and a human who approves something other than what executes — the yes rests on the render, not the hash, so an unfaithful render defeats the approval gate without ever tripping it. Not hypothetical: Grok Bot self-reports this exact shape, its approval render being model-authored prose that "can summarize, omit, or be precise", quality being "the model's judgment that turn". If OIKONOMOS accepts a caller-supplied free-text render it inherits the identical weakness at the identical point. AT REVIEW establish which GB built: (a) render DERIVED deterministically from the payload — the Synthesis comment's "destination + content/param diff" points this way — in which case OIKONOMOS is genuinely ahead and an ADR merely ratifies it; or (b) render accepted as caller-supplied text, in which case the gate is only as honest as its caller and that must close before TASK-016 wires the broker. An ADR is owed either way (docs/decisions/** protected, ORCH-authored, same shape as ADR-003) BEFORE TASK-015 and TASK-016, the consumers. If (b), the fix probably belongs with TASK-015's invalidation work rather than as rework here — GB built to the AC as written, so the AC is the defect, not the builder.

So the digest binds payload→execution while render→payload stays unbound, which is exactly the exposure ADR-004 exists to close. **THIS IS NOT REWORK AND NOT A FAULT OF GB.** AC1 requires only that the row *carries* digest, render, destination, nonce and expiry, and carrying it is precisely what was built; ADR-004 was authored after this session had already started and could not reach it. The acceptance criterion is the defect, not the builder — I recorded that position in the pre-review note above and in ADR-004's Consequences section BEFORE seeing the implementation, and I am holding to it now that the finding is confirmed. Remediation is already sequenced onto TASK-015, which carries an explicit ADR-004 acceptance criterion, and TASK-016 carries a matching one forbidding the broker from composing, accepting or passing a render. Ordering works out: 013 -> 014 -> 015 -> 016, so the signature changes in 015 BEFORE the broker wires it in 016. The reviewer should therefore NOT hold this task on the render; the remaining review question is whether AC1-AC5 are met as written. ### TASK-014
**Title:** OIK-022 — packages/approvals: verify + atomic consume (N8) ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §3, §4, §6; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-022; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §4.3; docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md CAN-06
**Owned_Paths:** packages/approvals/src/**, packages/approvals/test/**, packages/approvals/vitest.config.ts, packages/db/src/approvals.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-013
**Description:** **ORCH assignment 2026-08-15T13:40Z (protocol §8, from REVIEW.md evidence): GB.** Protected path, so GB or CX only, NEVER S5. Two reasons for GB over CX: on the evidence GB is 6 reviews / 6 first-pass approvals / 0 rework while CX is 10 / 5 / 3, and this is the single most security-critical function in the platform so far — that record is the one to spend on it. Second, CX authors TASK-013 in this same territory, and having a different unit write the consume statement puts fresh eyes on the invariant rather than the same reading twice. Sequenced behind TASK-013 by Depends_On, so the shared territory is never concurrent. **ORCH territory grant 2026-08-15T14:05Z:** `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` is in your Owned_Paths. TASK-020 creates that file with `insertApproval` + `getApprovalByNonce` and deliberately does NOT write the consume statement — you author `consumeApproval` there yourself. The reason is specific: the atomic-consume invariant and the concurrency test that proves it must be authored together, because a consume statement written by one task and tested by another is exactly the seam a fail-open defect survives (the same failure shape ADR-003 records for the max_tier ceiling/floor trap). You may modify the two functions TASK-020 delivered only if your tests show them wrong — say so explicitly in Progress_Notes if you do. Territorially safe: the audit chain (TASK-011/012) owns `packages/db/src/auditEvents.ts`, never this file, and the approvals chain TASK-013→014→015 is serialised by Depends_On so it is never concurrent with itself. The single most security-critical function in the platform so far. Consumption is **one atomic SQL statement** — the exact form is pinned in Handover §4.3 (`UPDATE approvals SET status='consumed', consumed_at=now() WHERE nonce=$1 AND status='granted' AND expires_at>now() AND consumed_at IS NULL`) — and the action runs **only if the row count is 1**. Any read-then-write shape, any transaction that checks and then updates, any application-level lock is a defect regardless of whether the test suite catches it: the invariant is that two concurrent consumers of the same nonce produce exactly one success at the *database* level. The concurrency test must be genuinely concurrent (parallel connections racing the same nonce), not sequential calls that merely look like a race. Sequenced behind TASK-013 in the same territory.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Concurrent consume attempts on one nonce yield exactly one success — proven by a DB-level test with genuinely parallel connections (WBS OIK-022 "Concurrent consume attempts ⇒ exactly one success (DB-level test)")
- [ ] Consumption is one atomic statement whose row count gates the action; row count 1 or the action does not run (N8, Handover §4.3)
- [ ] Expiry is honoured — an expired but still `granted` approval cannot be consumed (WBS OIK-022 "expiry honoured")
- [ ] Replay of a consumed nonce is denied and leaves `status='consumed'` unchanged (ADR-001 CAN-06)
- [ ] Integration tests pass against the compose Postgres; skip cleanly without `DATABASE_URL`

Barrel index.ts not touched (out of territory); persistence-surface reservation test now fails as expected.

**Review_Findings:** —
- APPROVED first-pass (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T15:10Z; protected path, author GB / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule satisfied). THE STRONGEST WORK ON THIS PROJECT SO FAR, and the verification was adversarial rather than confirmatory. THE PINNED SQL IS VERBATIM - I compared it clause by clause MYSELF against Handover 4.3 before delegating, and CONSUME_APPROVAL_SQL (packages/db/src/approvals.ts:212) is character-identical apart from the trailing semicolon: SET status='consumed', consumed_at=now(), WHERE nonce=$1 AND status='granted' AND expires_at>now() AND consumed_at IS NULL. No missing predicate, no extra predicate, no altered column or literal. Single pool.query, no BEGIN/COMMIT, no SELECT, no FOR UPDATE, no advisory lock; RETURNING is appended but changes neither which rows match nor the single-statement property. TWO MUTATION PROOFS, which is what separates this from a suite that merely passes: (1) dropping `status='granted' AND expires_at>now()` from the statement made three integration tests fail, so every predicate is LOAD-BEARING rather than decorative; (2) replacing the implementation with a check-then-update (SELECT, 20ms delay, unconditional UPDATE) made the parallel test fail Expected 1 / Received 9 - the concurrency test genuinely DETECTS the read-then-write shape the spec forbids. AC1 the race is real: 16 promises are constructed inside Array.from and left in flight before any await (not TASK-013's Promise.resolve costume), each consumeApproval opens its OWN Pool so these are 16 separate physical connections, and a second tighter test pre-establishes the connections to remove setup jitter; both assert EXACTLY ONE success and RACERS-1 zeros. AC2 row count gates the action - rowCount 1 is the only path carrying an approval (discriminated union, so a caller cannot reach the row on denial), rowCount ?? 0 defaults to deny on pg's nullable field, and rowCount > 1 THROWS rather than proceeding. AC3 expiry honoured, asserted with state (status still 'granted', consumedAt still null). AC4 replay denied AND UNCHANGED - the test asserts consumedAt timestamp EQUALITY, which catches a replay that silently re-stamps the row rather than merely checking status. AC5 37/37 under isolated pg16, 25 passed / 12 skipped without DATABASE_URL, clean skips. Quality: zero catch blocks (try/finally for cleanup only), every statement parameterised, nonce UUID-validated before the pool opens with a proven consumeCalls===0 on rejection, no credentials. TASK-013 REGRESSION CHECK CLEAN: issue.test.ts and helpers.ts are ADDITIVE ONLY - two lines adding a consume member to inline stubs so they satisfy the widened interface, zero deletions, zero assertion changes, all 9 pre-existing tests still pass. Territory clean, c8b9872 preflight in the committed dossier, zero PLAN.md commits. It is nonetheless production code depending on a sibling package's BUILD OUTPUT by relative path - it will break under bundling or any packaging that does not preserve that layout, and it silently depends on build ordering. RE-SCOPED to TASK-015 with the barrel grant needed to fix it properly. ### TASK-015
**Title:** OIK-023 + OIK-024 — approvals: invalidation on payload mutation, expiry sweeper ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §3, §4; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-023, OIK-024; docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md CAN-07
**Owned_Paths:** packages/approvals/src/**, packages/approvals/test/**, packages/approvals/vitest.config.ts, packages/db/src/index.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-014
**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5**). Two closely-related finishers, kept in one task because they share the same territory and neither is a full session alone. (1) OIK-023: if the action payload changes after approval was granted, the recomputed digest no longer matches, the approval transitions to `invalidated`, and a new approval is required (ADR-001 CAN-07 — this is the defence against "approve a harmless action, then swap the payload"). (2) OIK-024: an idempotent sweeper moving expired `pending` approvals to `expired`. Idempotent means running it twice changes nothing the second time, and running it concurrently with itself does not double-transition.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T15:10Z (ORCH), re-scoped from TASK-014 - my scoping consequence, not GB's defect.** `packages/approvals/src/store.ts` currently reaches the db implementation by FILESYSTEM PATH - `new URL("../../db/dist/approvals.js", import.meta.url)` plus a dynamic import - because I granted TASK-014 `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` but not the barrel, so `consumeApproval` could not be exported properly. GB handled the constraint correctly (documented, fails closed when packages/db is unbuilt) but it is production code depending on a sibling package's BUILD OUTPUT by relative path: it breaks under bundling or any packaging that does not preserve that layout, and it silently depends on build ordering. **NARROW GRANT:** `packages/db/src/index.ts` is added to your Owned_Paths for the sole purpose of exporting `consumeApproval` (and its result type) from the barrel. Then switch `store.ts` - and the integration tests that import `../../db/src/approvals.js` across the package boundary - to import from `@oikonomos/db`. Add nothing else to the barrel.
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T14:50Z (ORCH), per ADR-004:** `action_render` is DERIVED from the same canonical payload the digest covers and is never caller-supplied. If TASK-013 shipped a render parameter, remove it here and compute the render in `packages/shared` beside `actionDigest` from the identical canonical object (N10 — one canonicalization, not two). Carry the discriminating test ADR-004 §5 requires: a change to ANY digest-covered field must change the render, which fails for any implementation where the render is decorative, constant, or caller-controlled. Rationale: the digest binds payload→execution but NOT render→payload, so a render that is misleading at issuance yields a valid digest, a clean consume and a complete audit trail of an action the human was never shown. This sits with you rather than as TASK-013 rework because TASK-013's AC was written without the constraint — the criterion was the defect, not the builder.
- [ ] Digest mismatch after grant ⇒ status becomes `invalidated` and a new approval is required (WBS OIK-023; ADR-001 CAN-07)
- [ ] An invalidated approval cannot subsequently be consumed, even with a valid nonce
- [ ] Expired `pending` approvals transition to `expired`; the sweeper is idempotent across repeated and concurrent runs (WBS OIK-024)
- [ ] TASK-013 and TASK-014 tests still pass unchanged

ADR-004 is mandatory reading alongside ADR-003 — this is the second trap on this path where a value that looks like a harmless parameter silently defeats a control (ADR-003: a ceiling passed as a floor fails OPEN; ADR-004: a caller-supplied render fails the human).

- REWORK (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-16T10:40Z; protected path packages/broker, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). **READ THE POSITIVES FIRST — ALL SIX STATED ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ARE MET, and your ADR-003 handling is the best single piece of work on this project so far.** You did not merely avoid the trap, you made it structurally hard to fall into: `RoleGrantCeiling` is a DISTINCT TYPE, deliberately not named `roleGrantOverride`, carrying a comment that names the floor/ceiling inversion. `roleGrantOverride` is never passed; the ceiling is gated explicitly at the boundary. That is type-level prevention instead of a comment saying be careful, and it is the right answer to a trap that type-checks, runs and fails open. I verified the §4.1 contract MYSELF, character for character — all eight request fields including the nested agentRef shape, and all three response variants with identical names, types and optionality. And the negative test GENUINELY BITES, proven by two mutations: (M1) the trap as ADR-003 describes it killed 2 of 6 tests with the fail-open visible in the diff (`role.tier_ceiling` became `approval_pending` — the T1-capped role resolving to max(T3,T1)=T3); (M2) the subtler variant, keeping the gate but ALSO passing the ceiling as override, killed 1. Every request audits including all deny reasons, with no early return skipping the write; `capability.unregistered` matches §4.2 verbatim; and a grep for local `UPDATE approvals`/consume/canonical JSON/sha256 returns NO MATCHES — delegation is real.
- **[BLOCKING 1 — A MISSING ROLE GRANT FAILS OPEN. This is the same defect class ADR-003 exists to prevent, one branch over.]** `packages/broker/src/index.ts:137` reads `if (roleGrant !== null && exceedsCeiling(...))`. I verified the consequence directly rather than taking it second-hand: when `getRoleGrant` returns null — the role has NO GRANT AT ALL for this capability — every role check is skipped and the request proceeds to allow (T0–T2) or to the approval path (T3). Synthesis §5.1:199 defines the table in its own comment as "which role may request which capability"; absence of a row means the role MAY NOT. So a role with an INSUFFICIENT grant is correctly denied while a role with NO grant sails through — the permission check is defeated by having fewer permissions. **Nothing in your test fixture covers it: `test:34` always returns a grant.** This is not in the stated ACs and THAT GAP IS MY SPEC FAILURE, NOT YOURS — but CLAUDE.md non-negotiable #3, "Fail closed", is a standing platform rule that binds every task without needing restatement in an AC, and this is the broker, which Synthesis:146 calls the load-bearing wall. FIX: absent grant ⇒ deny, audited, with a test.
- **[BLOCKING 2 — T4_irreversible is allowed. This contradicts an explicit sentence in a spec this task references.]** `:62` `const APPROVAL_TIER: RiskTier = "T3_external"` and `:156` `if (tierRank(tier) < tierRank(APPROVAL_TIER))`. T4 outranks T3, so it falls into the approval branch and is ALLOWED once a nonce consumes. Synthesis §4:146 states in as many words: "Tier 3 blocks until an approval object is granted; **Tier 4 is denied**", and D2 at :31 is "Tier-4 deny-by-default", marked KEEP and core of Phase 0. Handover §4.1/§4.2 are silent on the tier-to-action mapping, so under CLAUDE.md document precedence Synthesis governs and there must be a T4 deny branch BEFORE the approval path. There is none and no test. FIX: T4 ⇒ deny regardless of nonce, audited, with a test proving a valid nonce does NOT rescue it.

Expected 2 or more, but got 1." The real `verifyAndConsume(nonce, deps, action?)` takes deps THIRD-position-shifted against your `(nonce, action)`. So a caller casting past the mismatch passes your digest-binding `action` into the DEPS position, leaving the real `action` undefined — and `packages/approvals/src/consume.ts:62` reads `if (action !== undefined)`, meaning **digest verification is silently SKIPPED and a payload-mutated approval consumes cleanly.** That is N10 defeated by a signature mismatch. All six tests mock your invented shape, so they prove nothing about the real contract. FIX: make the ports match the real exported signatures and bind at least one test against the genuine function rather than a mock of it. This must close before OIK-084 wires the HTTP surface.

Related: Synthesis:146 says "Tier 2 passes only under a role's standing policy"; you allow T2 unconditionally. Flag rather than fix if you judge it a separate task — but say so explicitly.
- [NON-BLOCKING] The approval-pending test asserts `toMatchObject({decision, reason, auditEventId})` and never asserts `approvalId` at all — the field a human needs to action the approval is guaranteed only by the type. Assert it non-empty. Also `updatedInput` is never emitted on any path (contract-compliant, since optional, but currently decorative), and the resolver-deny branch at `:151-153` is unreachable because the capability is fetched BY NAME and then handed to the resolver as a single-element array.
- NOTE ON FAIRNESS AND SEQUENCE: I want it on record that you met everything I asked for, and that the two fail-open findings sit in the space between my ACs and the platform's standing rules. I am still sending it back rather than filing follow-ups, for one reason: this is the broker, the single point every tool call passes through, and the next work on this path is the OIK-039 canaries and the G-GOV gate. A fail-open composition point must not reach a governance gate carried as a ticket — it would be exactly the thing a gate is meant to catch, arriving pre-approved. The fixes are small and on the same branch. Resume on task/TASK-016-cx (tip b709980, retained).

(FIX 3) The ports no longer hand-declare a shape, they ALIAS the real ones (`issueApproval: typeof issueApproval`, `verifyAndConsume: typeof verifyAndConsume`), which makes structural drift impossible by construction rather than merely corrected once. Assignability was PROVEN, not asserted: a temporary file assigning the concrete adapter to the port type compiled clean under `--strict --module nodenext`, while a control line asserting the OLD `(nonce, action)` shape produced exactly the expected `TS2322 … 'deps' and 'action' are incompatible` — so the old declaration really was non-assignable and the new one really is not. The digest-binding `action` now lands in the genuine third slot, reaching `consume.ts:62`, closing the N10 defeat.

### TASK-017
**Title:** OIK-028/029/030 — broker: idempotency, fail-closed, capability kill switch ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** docs/decisions/ADR-007-replay-window-semantics.md (NEW — read first, it decides the TTL question for you); specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-028, OIK-029, OIK-030; docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md R2, R3, CAN-04, CAN-08
**Owned_Paths:** packages/broker/src/**, packages/broker/test/**, packages/broker/vitest.config.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-016
**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5**). The three properties that make the broker trustworthy under failure, sequenced behind TASK-016 in the same territory. (1) OIK-028 idempotency per `toolUseId`: L1 and L3 hitting the broker for the same call produce **one** audit event and one decision (ADR-001 R2, CAN-08) — the decision must be replayed, not recomputed, or a policy change mid-call could yield two different answers for one tool use. (2) OIK-029 fail-closed: timeout >10s, HTTP 500, and a malformed body each deny **and** audit (ADR-001 R3, CAN-04) — an unreachable broker that fails open is the whole threat model realised. (3) OIK-030 kill switch: `capabilities.enabled=false` denies immediately with no restart required, so the switch must be read per request rather than cached at startup.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Same `toolUseId` via both L1 and L3 ⇒ exactly one audit event and one decision, with the second call replaying the first (WBS OIK-028; ADR-001 R2, CAN-08)
- [ ] Timeout >10s, 500, and malformed body each ⇒ deny plus an audit event, tested independently (WBS OIK-029; ADR-001 R3, CAN-04)
- [ ] `capabilities.enabled=false` denies immediately, verified without restarting the process (WBS OIK-030 "no restart required")
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-16T11:20Z (ORCH), carried from the TASK-016 re-review — an internal dependency throw is the same failure class as OIK-029's transport failures, one layer in.** An exception raised by `getCapability`, `getRoleGrant`, `verifyAndConsume` or `recordDecision` must produce a DENY and an audit event, not an unhandled rejection. Today the handler simply propagates and NO audit event is written, so a database blip is currently the one failure mode that leaves no trace — test each dependency throwing independently. **Review_Findings:** —
- REWORK (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-16T13:05Z; protected path packages/broker, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). **THE FAIL-CLOSED WORK IS GENUINELY GOOD AND ELEVEN OF ELEVEN MUTATIONS BEHAVED AS EXPECTED — this suite has real teeth.** AC3 kill switch is read per request with the interface documented against caching, and the no-restart property is tested the hard way, flipping enabled between two calls in the SAME process with a distinct toolUseId (reusing it would have replayed — you saw that trap and avoided it). AC4 internal-dependency-throw is handled for all four dependencies, and **you solved the ordering trap I flagged, correctly and explicitly**: when `recordDecision` is itself what threw, `audit()` converts it to a typed `AuditUnavailableError` rather than a fake success, the outer catch returns a DENY with an explicit `auditEventId: "unavailable"` sentinel, and `failClosed` carries the same guard so the deny-path audit cannot re-enter the loop. It denies, the behaviour is named in code AND in the test title, and it is observable downstream instead of silently allowing. That is the right answer. AC5 now touches the REAL `issueApproval`/`verifyAndConsume` with only the store port faked — the correct seam — and it RAN rather than skipping (19 tests, zero skipped). AC6 regression is clean: the entire removed-line set across the branch is ONE import line, all nine TASK-016 tests are byte-identical, and both the ADR-003 and T4 mutations still kill tests. 14 of 14 return sites audit.
- **[BLOCKING — THE REPLAY CACHE IS A LIVE CROSS-TENANT AUTHORISATION BYPASS. This is the most serious defect found on this project.]** `packages/broker/src/index.ts:201,205` key the cache on `request.toolUseId` ALONE. I verified the key myself rather than take it second-hand: nothing in it identifies the requester — no `tenantId`, no `roleId`. The probe result is unambiguous: role A gets an allow; then `roleId: "attacker-role"` in `tenantId: "other-tenant"`, **with no role grant at all** — a request that recomputes to a deny — reuses the same `toolUseId` and **RECEIVES ROLE A'S ALLOW, with NO audit event written for it.** Two of this platform's non-negotiables fail at once: the request is authorised without a grant, and it passes through unaudited. `toolUseId` originates in the harness/agent payload — the UNTRUSTED side of the boundary — so this is attacker-selectable, not an accident of collision. The `WeakMap` gives no isolation whatsoever here: it is keyed on the `BrokerDependencies` composition, which in a real service is ONE object shared by every tenant; it only frees memory on teardown. **Note what has happened structurally, because it is the lesson rather than the bug: the idempotency layer you added in AC1 re-opens precisely the door that the role-grant check and the kill switch exist to close.** It is the same shape as non-negotiable #7 (ACL before similarity, never after) — authorisation defeated by a caching layer sitting in front of it. FIX: key on `${tenantId}\0${roleId}\0${toolUseId}`. The fix is drop-in — applied as a probe, the full suite stayed green at 19/19 — and **that is itself the gap: the suite cannot currently tell the two apart, so the test is the part that must ship.** Required: a test asserting a second role does NOT inherit the first's decision AND that its request is independently audited.
- **[BLOCKING — the cache is unbounded, and this quietly reintroduces the staleness AC3 forbids]** `new Map()` per composition at `:198`, `cache.set` at `:205`, and I grepped: there is no `delete`, no eviction, no TTL, no size cap anywhere in the file. Two distinct problems. (a) Memory: a long-lived broker process accumulates an entry for every tool use it has ever seen, each holding a resolved promise and a response object. (b) **The replay window never closes** — verified with 5000 distinct `toolUseId`s, after which the oldest still replayed its original ALLOW even though policy had since been flipped to deny. AC3's entire point is "denies immediately, no restart required"; an unbounded replay cache means that for any `toolUseId` already seen, a kill-switch flip, a revoked role grant or a changed capability tier is silently ignored forever. Bound it with an LRU cap or a TTL tied to the tool-use lifetime, with a test proving an entry stops replaying.
- [NON-BLOCKING, same pass — AC5's headline assertion does not discriminate] The mutated-payload test asserts `resolves.toEqual({ consumed: false, rowCount: 0 })`, but the in-memory fake's `consume` returns `{ rowCount: 0, approval: null }` UNCONDITIONALLY — running the identical flow with an UNMUTATED payload produces the same `consumed:false`. The only line actually proving digest binding is the side-effect assertion `expect(row.status).toBe("invalidated")` (mutated -> invalidated, unmutated -> stays granted). The AC is met, but by the quieter line, and a reader would reasonably believe the headline assertion is the proof. Make the fake's `consume` digest-aware, or add the unmutated control case so the two outcomes visibly differ.
- [NON-BLOCKING — record it, do not implement it here] **The >10s timeout from non-negotiable #3 is enforced NOWHERE in this repository.** A grep for the constant finds only `packages/db/src/database.ts` `idleTimeoutMillis` and unrelated test lines. Your source is honest about this (the comment defers it to the OIK-084 HTTP adapter) and what you built is correct — GIVEN a timeout signal, the broker denies and audits. But the test named "timeout over 10 seconds" merely constructs `new BrokerFailure("broker.timeout")`, so the "10 seconds" is a STRING LABEL, not an assertion, and a reader scanning the suite would conclude the clause is covered when no code in the repo enforces a deadline. Rename the test to say what it proves, and I will carry the deadline itself as an explicit OIK-084 obligation rather than leaving it implied by a comment.
- [NON-BLOCKING, cosmetic but safety-relevant] The body of `decidePreToolUse` (`:218-317`) is not indented inside its own `try`, which makes the fail-closed scope hard to see when reading. Lint accepts it; it is still the single most safety-relevant brace in the package and it should be obvious.
- NOTE ON THE VERDICT: the CI red observed during review was `controls-live` reporting an undrained TASK-024 control block — ORCH orchestration state in the main checkout, NOT your defect, and I have drained it. Nothing in this rework is a criticism of the task's shape; three of the four ACs are met outright and the fourth is met in substance. The blocking items are both in the ONE mechanism this task added that had no prior art in the file, which is exactly where a new door gets left open. Resume on task/TASK-017-cx (tip b8b95f7, retained).
- REWORK, round 2 (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-16T14:10Z; protected path packages/broker, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). **THE CROSS-TENANT LEAK IS GENUINELY AND DEMONSTRABLY FIXED — that was the serious one and it is closed.** The key is now `${tenantId}\0${roleId}\0${toolUseId}`, the new test asserts BOTH halves I required (the second role does not inherit the ALLOW, and it is independently audited via `recordDecision` twice), and the decisive mutation BITES: reverting the key to `toolUseId` alone reproduces the exact leak in the diff (`deny/role.grant_missing` becomes `allow/T1_draft`). An independent probe confirms the attacker role now denies AND writes an audit event carrying its own tenant. The TTL also bites (removing the expiry check fails a test), and it uses `vi.useFakeTimers()` rather than real sleeps, so it is neither slow nor flaky. AC5 discrimination is properly fixed — the fake `consume` is now state-aware and there is an unmutated CONTROL case producing a genuinely different result (`consumed:true/rowCount:1` vs `consumed:false/rowCount:0` plus `status:"invalidated"`), so the assertion now discriminates by construction. The misleading test name is fixed. 21/21 pass, and all four regression traps still kill tests (ADR-003, T4, kill-switch caching, and the `recordDecision`-throws ordering trap still denies with the `unavailable` sentinel).
- **[BLOCKING 1 — the LRU cap has NO liveness assertion, and CLAUDE.md makes that a hard reject]** Deleting the entire eviction block leaves the suite at **21/21 passing**, and `pnpm lint` does not even flag the now-unused `REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES`. The eviction CODE is correct — a behavioural probe after 1200 tool uses showed the oldest key evicted and the newest still cached — but correct-and-unguarded is precisely the state this project has now found configured-but-inert eight times. CLAUDE.md is unambiguous: "Every mechanical control ships a liveness assertion... Reviewers reject its absence like a missing test." A memory bound is a mechanical control. Add a test that fails when eviction is inert.
- **[BLOCKING 2 — an in-flight decision can be evicted, and one toolUseId then yields TWO contradictory verdicts]** The eviction loop walks `cache.keys()` in insertion order with **no check whether the entry's promise has settled**, so a request still awaiting `getCapability`/`verifyAndConsume` is evicted like any other once the cap is reached. Probe P3, with a victim request held pending and the cache flooded with 1100 tool uses: **2 capability lookups, 2 `verifyAndConsume` calls on the same nonce, 2 audit events, and two CONTRADICTORY responses for one `toolUseId`** — the first caller received `deny/approval_not_granted`, the second `allow/T3_external`. That is ADR-001 R2 / CAN-08 violated directly. Non-negotiable #8 saves you from the worst of it — the real consume is one atomic SQL statement, so only one attempt can ever get row count 1 and there is NO double side-effect on the nonce — but which caller receives the ALLOW is decided by a race, and the audit log records two conflicting verdicts for a single tool use. FIX: never evict an unsettled entry (track a `settled` flag set in `.finally()`), or refuse to evict entries younger than the TTL and let the TTL alone bound growth.
- **[BLOCKING 3 — the TTL/AC1 tension is real, and I have now DECIDED it for you: read ADR-007 before touching this]** Your TTL silently narrows ADR-001 R2: a probe confirms 1 audit event within the window and **2 after expiry**, with the decision recomputed. That narrowing is defensible, but the justification offered ("expires with the hook timeout window") is not, and **the error is originally MINE — I floated that same defence in review before testing it.** It conflates two unrelated quantities: non-negotiable #3's 10s is the BROKER RESPONSE DEADLINE (how long one call may take), whereas R2's window is the L1→L3 INTERVAL (how long between the hook returning and `canUseTool` firing). Nothing bounds the second by the first; L1 can answer in 50ms and L3 can fire later behind a slow model turn. **I have written ADR-007 to settle it, because this is a document-precedence conflict and `docs/` is outside your territory — you could not have resolved it even if you had spotted it.** ADR-007 accepts the time-bounded guarantee and explains why a recompute is not a safety hole (it re-runs every check, so it cannot yield an authorisation current policy would deny — the exposure is a duplicate audit entry, never a bypass), and it rejects the deterministic-post-expiry-deny alternative as unimplementable within a bounded cache. WHAT YOU MUST DO: re-derive the constant from the L1→L3 interval, comment it citing ADR-007 and NOT non-negotiable #3, and add an explicit test for post-expiry recomputation so the narrowing is a demonstrated property rather than an emergent one.
- [NON-BLOCKING, same pass] Tenant isolation is not independently tested: the test flips `roleId` and `tenantId` TOGETHER, so role alone carries the assertion, and a mutation dropping `tenantId` from the key passes the entire suite 21/21. `tenantId` is in the key but nothing holds it there. Vary them separately. (ADR-007 §4 now requires this.)
- [NON-BLOCKING, record only — not yours to fix here] `getRoleGrant(roleId, capabilityId)` takes no `tenantId`, so tenant scoping of the grant lookup rests entirely on the caller's adapter — a latent version of the same hazard one layer down. - REWORK, round 3 (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-16T15:40Z; protected path packages/broker, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). **ROUND 2'S THREE BLOCKING ITEMS ARE GENUINELY CLOSED AND I HAD EACH PROVEN BY MUTATION.** The LRU cap now has a real liveness assertion — deleting the eviction block fails a named test where round 2 passed 21/21 — and the test forces >1024 entries with pure stubs in 28ms rather than by sleeping. In-flight eviction is fixed and the probe numbers invert cleanly: where round 2 measured 2 capability lookups, 2 verifyAndConsume calls on one nonce, 2 audit events and two CONTRADICTORY verdicts, the same probe now measures 1/1/1 with identical responses. Post-expiry recompute is explicitly tested per ADR-007 §4, and that test proves the second audit event AND that the verdict follows current policy — which incidentally demonstrates §2.3's argument that a recompute re-runs every check. Tenant and role are now guarded INDEPENDENTLY: the combined test was split, and removing either component from the key fails its own test, where round 2's combined test caught neither. All four standing traps still kill tests. 24/24, territory clean, no PLAN.md edit, and no assertion was removed or weakened — the two changed tests were restructured strictly stronger.
- **[BLOCKING — the ONE new guarantee this round introduced is the one with no test, which is the exact pattern that has now cost this task three rounds]** The code claims an in-flight decision is replayed even past the window: `if (!replay.settled || replay.expiresAt > Date.now())`. **Dropping the `!replay.settled ||` clause leaves the suite green at 24/24.** I had it verified that the guarantee is real — with fake timers, a victim held in-flight while the clock advances 600s is still replayed, 1 capability lookup, responses equal — so once again the CODE IS CORRECT AND THE TEST IS BLIND. ADR-007 §4 names never-splitting an in-flight tool use as a requirement, and the past-expiry half of it is undefended: the existing in-flight test never advances the clock, so it only ever exercises the `expiresAt > now` branch. FIX: extend that test to advance fake timers past the window before re-submitting. This is small, and it is the last instance of a pattern you have otherwise now fixed three times.
- **[RESOLVED BY ME, NOT BY YOU — the window derivation]** `L1_TO_L3_REPLAY_WINDOW_MS = 60_000` cites ADR-007 and correctly disclaims non-negotiable #3, which fixes the mislabelling. But it is not DERIVED — the reviewer's plain assessment, which I agree with, is that it is a bigger number than 10s with a corrected label. **I have amended ADR-007 with §2.4a rather than send the question back**, because this is an architectural decision and `docs/` is outside builder territory, so you could not have settled it even having spotted the gap. §2.4a fixes the window at 60s and states the basis on both sides — roughly two orders of magnitude of headroom over the sub-second harness handoff so scheduling jitter cannot cross it, and short enough that a revoked grant or flipped kill switch still takes effect promptly for an already-seen toolUseId — and it records that this is an ASSUMPTION to be re-derived from measurement when OIK-084 can emit the real L1→L3 distribution. YOUR ACTION IS NARROW: point the comment at ADR-007 §2.4a, and note the OIK-084 measurement obligation in the dossier.
- [NON-BLOCKING] The `failClosed` sentinel return is untested: flipping it to ALLOW passes 24/24. It is reachable — a dependency throws AND the resulting deny's own audit write fails — and it is the last line of defence in the fail-closed chain. One test with a throwing `getCapability` plus a throwing `recordDecision` covers it.
- [NON-BLOCKING] The eviction comment's claim that a burst ">1,024 simultaneous requests may temporarily exceed the cap, then contracts safely" was MEASURED TRUE — 5,000 concurrent never-settling requests were all retained, then the cache contracted once they settled, so growth is bounded by peak in-flight concurrency rather than unbounded. But there is one case the comment does not cover: **an entry whose dependency hangs and never settles is pinned forever**, since there is no time-based sweep and the broker-side deadline lives in OIK-084, not here. That is the single way this cache is genuinely unbounded. Add a sentence, or carry it to TASK-017's OIK-084 successor.
- [COSMETIC, THIRD ASK] `decidePreToolUse`'s body is still not indented inside its own `try`. ESLint does not enforce it, so it will never be caught mechanically — which is precisely why it needs doing by hand. It is the most safety-relevant brace in the package.
- [MECHANICAL] Rebase before your next submission: ADR-007 is not in this branch's merge base (it landed on master at 8d49b14, and §2.4a is later still), so the document you are being judged against is not visible from the branch.
- APPROVED on round 4 (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-16T16:20Z; protected path packages/broker, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). **THE BLOCKING ITEM IS CLOSED WITH A TEST THAT IS GENUINELY HARD TO FAKE, and I had it checked for the specific way it could have been worthless.** A test that advances the clock but lets the victim promise settle first would exercise the `expiresAt > now` branch, pass, look correct, and defend nothing. This one does not do that: the victim's `getCapability` returns a promise resolved only AFTER the second submit, and there is no `await` between `vi.advanceTimersByTime(60_001)` and the re-submission, so the entry provably cannot settle across the advance. It asserts on an unsettled entry. It also composes both hazards — 1,024 settled entries are inserted first, so the victim must survive LRU eviction AND window expiry. And it fails on the RIGHT evidence: the mutation is caught by the `recordDecision` count, i.e. by the second audit event for one toolUseId, which IS the CAN-08 violation rather than a proxy for it.
- ALL TEN MUTATIONS BITE. The eight standing traps (ADR-003 ceiling-as-floor, T4 deny branch, kill-switch caching, replay key reverted to toolUseId alone, tenant dropped, role dropped, eviction loop deleted, entries never expiring) each fail a named test, with tenant and role each caught by their OWN dedicated test rather than only by the combined key. Plus the two sentinel probes. The non-blocking `failClosed` gap was also closed unprompted — flipping that fallback to ALLOW now fails `fails closed when both a dependency and the resulting denial audit fail`, which is the last line of the fail-closed chain and required a nested-failure test (dependency throws AND the resulting deny's own audit write fails).
- **NO SILENT WEAKENING, WHICH IS WHAT I WAS MOST CONCERNED ABOUT FOUR ROUNDS IN.** I had the test diff read for REMOVED and MODIFIED assertions, not just additions. Result: zero removed, zero modified. The pre-existing in-flight test was renamed and wrapped in fake timers, and all four of its original assertions survive verbatim with identical values; the only insertion in its body is the clock advance, which strictly strengthens it. Production logic is byte-identical to round 3 apart from the comment change. 24 -> 25 tests, every previously-named test still present and verified by name against the prior set.
- The window comment now cites ADR-007 §2.4a and names ADR-001 R3 only to DISCLAIM it, which is the correct form and exactly what §2.4a instructs; the OIK-084 measurement obligation is recorded in the dossier pointing at the ADR rather than restating it. §4.1 union unchanged, all three shapes. 14 return sites, 12 auditing, the 2 exceptions being precisely the `unavailable` sentinels where auditing through the known-broken writer is impossible by construction — and the replay return deliberately writes no new event, which IS CAN-08 and is the property the M1 test guards. No local reimplementation of consume, canonical JSON or digest. Territory clean across four rounds, no PLAN.md edit. Merged --no-ff; **master verified GREEN on the FULL recursive suite** (broker 25/25, approvals 43+14 skipped, audit 24+11, services green) with the gate at 7/7.
- TWO ITEMS CARRIED RATHER THAN FORCING A FIFTH ROUND, both recorded here so they are not lost. (1) **The never-settling-entry pin is still undocumented** — an entry whose dependency promise never resolves is exempt from LRU eviction (it is unsettled) AND from expiry (it is replayed regardless), so it is pinned for the process lifetime with no time sweep. It is the one genuinely unbounded path in this cache, and it was measured, not theorised. Its natural home is OIK-084: the reason it is unbounded is that no broker-side deadline exists yet, and OIK-084 adds exactly that deadline, which bounds it automatically. Carry it there rather than patching a comment here. (2) `decidePreToolUse`'s body is still not indented inside its own `try` — asked four times, cosmetic, and ESLint will never catch it. Neither justifies another round against work where all ten mutations bite.
- NOTE FOR THE RECORD ON WHY THIS TOOK FOUR ROUNDS: every rejection was the same defect class — correct code, blind test — and never a wrong implementation. Rounds 1-3 each shipped a mechanism that worked and a suite that could not tell if it stopped working. That is precisely what CLAUDE.md's liveness rule exists to catch, and holding the line produced a broker whose ten most dangerous failure modes are each pinned by a test that fails when the mechanism is broken. The cost was three extra cycles; the alternative was an enforcement point with untested guards at the OIK-039 canaries and the G-GOV gate.

### TASK-018
**Title:** OIK-003 — CODEOWNERS + protected-path CI enforcement ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** medium
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §3; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E1 OIK-003; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §3 (protected paths); CLAUDE.md
**Owned_Paths:** .github/CODEOWNERS, .github/workflows/**, infra/ci/**
**Depends_On:** —
**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5** — infra/ci is protected). The protected-path rule is currently enforced by convention and review only; this makes it mechanical. Add `.github/CODEOWNERS` covering the protected paths from CLAUDE.md and Handover §3 (`packages/broker/**`, `packages/policy/**`, `packages/approvals/**`, `packages/harness-factory/**`, `infra/ci/**`, `docs/decisions/**`, all settings and subagent configs), plus a CI check that fails a PR touching a protected path without the required review. The repo has no remote yet, so acceptance is demonstrated locally the same way TASK-004 handled it: the check must be runnable as a script and prove both the catch and the pass. Note the honest limit — CODEOWNERS itself only takes effect on a GitHub remote, so the local script is what actually enforces this until the remote exists; say so in the README rather than implying coverage you do not have.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `.github/CODEOWNERS` covers every protected path listed in CLAUDE.md and Handover §3, with no path omitted
- [ ] A local check script fails when a diff touches a protected path without the required approval marker, and passes when it does not (WBS OIK-003 "Protected-path PR without review is blocked by CI", adapted to no-remote per TASK-004 precedent)
- [ ] Self-test proves both directions — the catch and the clean pass — as separate assertions
- [ ] README states plainly that CODEOWNERS is inert until a GitHub remote exists and the local script is the operative control until then
- [ ] Existing infra/ci self-tests (banned-modes, secret-scan) stay green
- [ ] **ADDED AT REWORK 2026-08-15T13:40Z (ORCH), scope addition — read the note:** the real gate runs in an automated local path, not only its self-test. `run-local.mjs` must invoke `protected-path-review.mjs` against a real diff, so that a protected-path change is actually caught in a no-remote checkout. Solve the wrinkle deliberately rather than by suppressing the check: run-local is itself routinely executed on branches that legitimately touch protected paths (this task's own branch does), so an unconditional failing run would make the local suite unusable for exactly the tasks that need it. Either resolve the base as the merge-base with the integration branch and pass the marker through, or make a protected-diff hit a distinct, explained non-zero state that a marker argument clears. State in the README which control is operative in a no-remote checkout.
- [ ] **ADDED AT REWORK 2026-08-15T13:40Z (ORCH), scope addition:** CODEOWNERS and the gate's `PROTECTED_PATHS` both cover `hooks/**`, `.claude/**` and `.codex/**`. This is a genuine widening of your original scope and not a reinterpretation of what you delivered — AC1 says "every protected path listed in CLAUDE.md", and I changed that list after reviewing your work (CLAUDE.md §"Protected paths", amended 2026-08-15). Your narrower reading of the old wording ("all settings and subagent configs" → `settings*.json` + `agents/**` + `.codex/**`) was correct for the text as it stood; the text was the defect. Rationale now recorded in CLAUDE.md: `hooks/` holds `territory-precommit.js`, the mechanical territory control from ADR-002 Amendment B, and `.claude/commands/**` defines the review procedure itself — under the old list the review standard could be edited without review.

### TASK-021
**Title:** OIK-003 follow-on — control liveness gate (ADR-005) ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** docs/decisions/ADR-005-control-liveness.md; docs/decisions/ADR-002-permission-bypass-ban-scope.md Amendment B; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E1 OIK-003
**Owned_Paths:** infra/ci/**
**Depends_On:** TASK-018, TASK-022
**Description:** **GB or CX only, never S5** (protected path infra/ci). Implements ADR-005. Seven controls in this repo were found configured-but-inert in one day, and three of the seven were caught by luck rather than by any mechanism. The shared property is that a working control and an inert one present IDENTICAL symptoms: config present, command exits 0, output plausible. ATLAS is the sharpest case — `pack` opens the index on every dispatch, updating its mtime, so it looked freshly written at the exact second of each dispatch while its contents were six hours and eight merges stale. Add a `controls-live` job to `infra/ci/run-local.mjs` asserting LIVENESS — that each control actually ran — as distinct from correctness, which the existing jobs already cover. **Read ADR-005 §2 before writing anything: each assertion must key on evidence the control emits BY DOING ITS JOB (a recorded scan timestamp, a drained queue, a coverage report, a rejected commit) and NEVER on the presence of config, a file's mtime, or an exit code from a command that may have no-opped.** An assertion keyed on an mtime would reproduce the exact ATLAS failure one layer up. Where a control cannot emit such evidence, say so in the README rather than inventing a proxy. Keep every check able to FAIL: a liveness assertion that cannot fail is the thing this task exists to eliminate.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `run-local.mjs` gains a `controls-live` job whose failure fails the run, covering: **(a) ATLAS index freshness — DESCOPED 2026-08-15T19:35Z to TASK-024, do not implement here;** (b) the territory pre-commit hook is installed and is ours; (c) the control queue is drained — read the MAIN CHECKOUT's `.devteam/control` resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, NOT the worktree's, because `.devteam/` is gitignored and therefore per-worktree while the queue that matters is the one the supervisor drains; an absent directory THERE is genuinely unobservable and must fail, an absent directory in a worktree is merely irrelevant (AMENDED 2026-08-15T21:25Z — ORCH failed to generalise the per-worktree `.devteam/` diagnosis from the ATLAS descope); (d) every unit in `autopilot.json`'s `builders.active` has a non-null `model`; (e) `packages/policy` coverage is actually COLLECTED on an ordinary test run, not merely configured; (f) no workspace package's compiled `dist/` is stale relative to its `src/`
- [ ] Each assertion is proven able to FAIL — a self-test induces the inert state for every check and asserts a non-zero result. This is the criterion that matters: a liveness check that cannot fail is indistinguishable from no check
- [ ] No assertion keys on a file mtime, on the presence of a config key alone, or on an exit code from a command that may have no-opped (ADR-005 §2). State in the README, per check, WHICH emitted evidence it reads
- [ ] Staleness is reported in judgeable units — commits behind, files missing — not a bare timestamp the reader must date-arithmetic (ADR-005 §5)
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T16:20Z (ORCH) - sixth check, found during TASK-015 review and a textbook instance of the class this task exists for.** Assert that no workspace package's compiled `dist/` is STALE relative to its `src/`. Why: `@oikonomos/db`'s `exports` map resolves to `./dist/index.js` and `dist/` is gitignored, so cross-package tests execute build output rather than source. Demonstrated consequence - a mutation to `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` went entirely UNDETECTED by the approvals suite until a rebuild, and moving `packages/db/dist` aside makes 10 of 13 approvals test files fail to load. A suite that appears to guard `src` while executing `dist` is exactly the configured-but-inert shape of ADR-005. Key the assertion on evidence, per ADR-005 section 2: compare newest source mtime against build-output mtime per package, or verify the build is current, and FAIL when a package's dist is older than its src. Report it in judgeable terms (which package, how far behind), not a bare timestamp.
- [ ] `infra/ci/README.md` documents the job, and states plainly that liveness checks are themselves controls that can rot
- [ ] Existing `run-local.mjs` jobs and the infra/ci self-tests stay green; the protected-path gate still fails this branch without a marker

- REWORK (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T21:05Z; protected path infra/ci, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule holds). READ THE POSITIVES FIRST, THEY ARE THE LARGER PART: the central claim of this task holds in form. All FIVE assertions genuinely induce failure in test-controls-live.mjs, and the self-test asserts BOTH the individual check status and the aggregate exit code, which is more than the AC asked. I independently broke FOUR of the five real conditions rather than trusting the fixtures — moved the installed .git/hooks/pre-commit, dropped a stray json into .devteam/control, nulled GB's model pin, and touched packages/shared/src to age its dist — and in every case the correct NAMED check flipped to FAIL. controls-live.mjs contains not a single try/catch and fails closed on spawn failure (`status ?? 1`) and on an unreadable autopilot.json. run-local integration is correct and BLOCKING, not advisory: the job's status is counted into `failed` and the process exits 1 unconditionally. The ATLAS descope is HONEST — 58 lines genuinely deleted, one remaining reference and it is the README explaining the deferral to TASK-024 with the correct reason (per-worktree gitignored index, refreshed only in the main checkout, no builder-worktree maintainer). Territory clean, all commits tagged, no authored PLAN.md edits.
- [BLOCKING 1 — the coverage check's verdict is decided by TERMINAL COLOUR, not by the control it observes] `controls-live.mjs:61` matches `/% Coverage report from v8[\s\S]*All files/i` against captured vitest stdout. Real vitest output interleaves ANSI escapes THROUGH that phrase — captured verbatim: `^[[34m % ^[[39m^[[2mCoverage report from ^[[22m^[[33mv8^[[39m` — so the literal sequence cannot match when colour is on. THE REVIEWER SAW THIS CHECK FAIL AND EXIT 1; I SAW IT PASS AND EXIT 0. Neither of us is wrong: the determinant is whether the spawned child believes it is on a TTY, which differs between harnesses. That is worse than a check that is always red or always green, because the gate's verdict depends on the terminal rather than on whether policy coverage was collected — the precise ADR-005 failure class, sitting inside the gate written to enforce ADR-005. FIX: strip ANSI before matching (or match a colour-immune anchor), and prove it by asserting the check against REAL captured output containing escapes, not a hand-written clean string.
- [BLOCKING 2 — the hook check keys on CONFIG PRESENCE, which ADR-005 §2 explicitly forbids] The check shells out to `install_git_hooks.ps1 -Verify`, and that verifier does nothing but `Test-Path` the hook file and match the `devteam-composed-pre-commit` marker inside it. The `[install-hooks] installed:` line it prints is derived purely from file state, so wrapping it in a command does not make it evidence — it is still "the presence of configuration" dressed as command output. ADR-005 §2 names the correct evidence for this control in as many words: **a rejected commit**. Nothing here observes that the hook has ever executed. A present hook with `core.hooksPath` redirected elsewhere, or a dispatch path using `--no-verify`, passes this check while the control is entirely inert. FIX: observe execution — e.g. attempt a commit that stages an out-of-territory path in a throwaway repo/worktree with the hook installed and assert it is REJECTED. That is the same shape as your own excellent TASK-022 bite-test.
- [BLOCKING 3 — the control-queue check FAILS OPEN on a directory that does not exist] `existsSync(controlDirectory) ? readdirSync(...) : []` returns `[]` for an absent directory, and `[]` means PASS. I verified in your worktree: `.devteam/control` DOES NOT EXIST there, so this check is currently passing by observing nothing at all. "A drained queue" is legitimate §2 evidence, but only if drained is distinguishable from never-existent — and here it is not. This is the single most on-the-nose instance of the anti-pattern this whole task exists to eliminate. FIX: treat an absent queue directory as UNOBSERVABLE and fail (or assert the directory exists as part of the check), and cover it in the self-test.
- [BLOCKING 4 — the dossier's final claim is false and must be corrected] Your 20:11Z entry states `node infra/ci/run-local.mjs --approval-marker fable-reviewed is fully green`. It was not green in the reviewer's environment, where controls-live failed on the coverage check and run-local exited 1. Given BLOCKING 1 this claim is environment-dependent rather than deliberately wrong, but Test_Evidence must be reproducible — this is the second time on this project that a pasted "green" could not be reproduced by the reviewer (TASK-007 was the first). Re-run after the fixes and record what the command actually prints, including which environment it was run in.
- [NON-BLOCKING — the gap that let BLOCKING 1 ship] The self-test drives `runLivenessChecks(root, supplied)` with hand-written evidence objects and never exercises a single REAL collector: `command()`, `workspacePackages()`, the actual `.devteam/control` read and the real coverage regex are all untested. That is precisely why a regex that cannot match real output passed its own suite. Worth one test per collector against genuine output.
- [NON-BLOCKING] The dist-freshness induced state hits the `distFiles.length === 0` early return, so the actual `oldestDist < latestSource` staleness arm is never exercised by any test — I proved it works by ageing packages/shared, but you should assert it. Also: `sourceFiles.length === 0` silently skips a package whose src vanished (fail-open), and `workspacePackages` scans only `packages` and `services` while CLAUDE.md names `apps/` as a source root too.
- [NON-BLOCKING] `command('powershell', ...)` is a hard Windows-and-PowerShell-5 dependency; on Linux/macOS this check fails closed with "verifier rejected the installed hook", which is a false accusation rather than an honest "cannot observe". Given the CI job runs `ubuntu-latest`, worth making the inability to observe explicit. Also `const here = ...` at line 15 is assigned and never used.

Three of the four blocking items are the same underlying mistake (a check that observes something other than the control), which is a genuinely hard thing to get right and is exactly why ADR-005 §2 spends a paragraph on it.
